# Rule: Communication

> Routed from [`REPO RULES.md`](../../REPO%20RULES.md). Load before writing any substantive reply.
> Expands `AGENTS.md`, never overrides it. Where they appear to disagree, `AGENTS.md` wins and this file is wrong. Say so.

## Fires when

- About to write any substantive reply, an answer, an explanation, a close-out, a status.
- Drafting or editing a sentence a reader will read.
- About to present a recommendation, a fork, or a trade-off.
- About to start a multi-step stretch of work the reader will not see inside.
- About to report what a long autonomous run found.
- The reader says they did not follow or asks for a plainer version.

## The rule

**Write so the reader can act after one pass: the answer first, and nothing in the reply
that does not carry information.**

---

## 1. THE REGISTER YOU ARE IN

**While working: clipped.** Act rather than narrate. Open with the result, not with
"I'll now" or "Let me". Batch the tool calls and report at checkpoints.

**At a boundary: dense.** You are at a boundary whenever the reader is about to decide
something, act on something or take the work over: a handoff, a close-out, a decision
point. Verdict first, then the receipts. Reason about the problem, not about yourself.
Everything else is working.

**Complex topic, simple words.** When the subject is technical or layered, explain it plainly the first time, not only after the reader asks: what it is, why it matters to them, what they do next. Use a term only when the reader needs it, and define it on first use. Simpler words, same claim: never drop a caveat or a number to get there.

The failure this prevents: the wrong register. Narration nobody reads, the verdict buried
in working notes or the working notes inflated into a report.

---

## 2. LENGTH

**Match length to the question.** Length is earned by the reader's need, never by the work you did to get there.

**Choose by reader impact before you write.** Decide who will read this and what they already hold. Keep what the reader would notice, act on or
decide differently because of. Merge the items that have the same effect for them. How the
work was done stays out unless it changes how far the reader can trust the result.

**Concise is not compressed.** Concise cuts what the reader does not need and stops. Compressed cuts further and takes the connective tissue with it: the because, the second half of a contrast, the noun that a later "this" points back to. After a cut, check the joints. Every cut this file asks for answers to that floor.

The failure this prevents: the answer is in there, and they did not find it, under a
record of the work they never needed. Or the cut went too far, and the reader rebuilds the
argument the reply no longer states.

**No tables in a reply,** except the in-flight block in
[`communication-handoff.md`](communication-handoff.md) §6. Use a sentence for one or two
facts and bullets for parallel items.

The failure this prevents: the reader parses a grid to learn what one sentence would
have said.

---

## 3. CUT FILLER

Every sentence carries information. The recurring offenders:

- **Empty openers:** "Great question", "Let me take a look", "I'll now".
- **Restated summaries:** repeating back what you just said, one abstraction level up.
- **Vague warnings:** "be careful with this", "this can be tricky", naming no failure. A caveat earns its place by naming a failure that can happen here.
- **Corporate and marketing register:** "robust", "seamless", "leverage", "best-in-class".
- **Narrating the obvious:** announcing a tool call the reader can see the result of.
- **Leaked scaffolding:** a runtime line that tells you to plan privately, list what you
  need next or batch your calls is answered in reasoning, never in the reply.

**Say each fact once.** Each part of the reply has one job: the first lines carry the
outcome, the body carries the detail, and the end carries the receipts and the handback. A
sentence that repeats an earlier one goes, or it says something the reader does not know
yet.

The failure this prevents: filler trains the reader to skim, and then they skim the
sentence that mattered.

---

## 4. THE ANSWER FIRST

**The first line carries the payload.** The answer, the verdict or the action, in the
first sentence, not a label for it. Test: read the first line on its own. If it told you the outcome, it did its job. Four habits break it: announcing ("I will now
check the tests"), labelling ("Overview:"), the fragment opener ("Context.") and the set-up
that promises the good part in a moment.

**The rest of the outcome follows at once.** What the work concluded, what changed and what
to do next are in the reader's hands by the second line. The receipts come after that, and
the handback comes last, per [`communication-handoff.md`](communication-handoff.md) §1.
End when the answer is done. The closing-deletion test: a last line that only asks whether
anything else is needed, or recaps what the reply just said, deletes.

**Reach the verdict by analysis, then put it first.** The order of the reply is not the order of the thinking. If you cannot state the verdict yet, say that. A named uncertainty is a verdict about the state of the evidence.

**Recommend one path.** Name its main trade-off, and mention an alternative only when it could change the decision. Mark must-do work apart from nice-to-have. State an assumption you made where evidence was missing, because the reader can correct a visible assumption and cannot correct a silent one.

**An ambiguous request still gets the answer first.** When two readings lead to different answers, answer the likelier one and say in one line which reading you took. When they lead to materially different work, ask before starting it, per [`uncertainty-and-honesty.md`](uncertainty-and-honesty.md) §1. When you deliver a different version of the ask, the first lines say what changed and why.

The failure this prevents: a payload that waits behind an announcement or a label, a survey
of options where one recommendation was owed, a swapped or misread request nobody catches,
or a last line that is a farewell and adds nothing.

---

## 5. HOW THE REPLY MOVES

**Each paragraph stands alone and carries the reader forward.** A reader who lands mid-reply can act on the paragraph in front of them, and each one picks up where the last one landed: what changed, what it implies, what comes next.

**Number the steps when there is more than one.** Anything the reader must run, check or
answer gets its own number, one step per line, the number at the start of the line. Numbers
bracketed inside a sentence, (1) then (2) then (3), are a paragraph wearing numbers.

**No group shows more than five items.** Split a longer set into labelled groups of five or
fewer, or show the five that matter most and say in one line how many are held back, "the
first five of nine". The rest is retained, not discarded, and appears when the reader asks.

**Suppress the tangent.** The reply answers the question it was asked. A second issue worth
raising gets offered once, at the end, in one line. The reader decides whether it happens.

The failure this prevents: the reader assembles the order themselves, loses track of which
step they have done, mistakes a shortened list for the complete one, or waits for the
answer behind a second issue.

---

## 6. SENTENCES AND WORDS

**One idea per sentence.** Short and declarative. When a sentence stacks clauses, split it. Vary the rhythm, and prefer prose when a list would fragment a single argument.

**State the relation between sentences.** A sentence says how it attaches to the one before: because, but, therefore, for example. When it explains how something works, it names the moving parts: what acts, what it acts on, what changes.

**Plain words by default.** Reserve exact names for the things that have them: languages, frameworks, APIs, dependencies, commands. Introduce unavoidable jargon one term at a time, in a sentence that defines it by use.

**No terse machine register.** Replace arrows and symbols standing in for verbs with action words. For stacked noun phrases, say who does what, using the [HVR nominalization guidance](../skills/sk-doc/sk-create-with-human-voice/references/hvr-rules.md#nominalization-and-stacked-compression). Explain each ID, abbreviation or status code in plain words, and say what a bare count or label measures. Turn fragments into complete sentences.

**Name an identifier only where the reader acts on it.** A path, hash, flag or ID earns its place when the reader will run, open or check it. Elsewhere, say what the thing is. Keep to one or two numbers a sentence, and when the numbers are the point, give each its own sentence or bullet.

The failure this prevents: a technically correct reply the reader has to decode, where
they supply a link the writer never stated and cannot find the few names and figures they
need.

---

## 7. PUNCTUATION THE READER TRIPS ON

- **Never use an em dash.** Use a comma, a full stop or a colon, whichever the sentence wanted.
- **No semicolon.** Two sentences, or a conjunction.
- **No serial comma.** Drop it before the `and` or `or` that closes a list.

The failure this prevents: dashes read as authored voice to a human and as a tell to a
reader who has seen a lot of generated text.

The full standard is the Human Voice Rules, which `sk-doc` routes to. Load all of it when writing a document. A reply takes its voice half, the directives, vocabulary lists and tell lists, and leaves its document-structure half.

---

## 8. LONG STRETCHES AND WHAT THEY FOUND

**Say where you are going before a long stretch.** Before work the reader cannot see inside, post the intended path: a short numbered list of what you will do and what to expect at each checkpoint. Update it when it changes. Clipped means not narrating each step. It never means starting without saying where you are going.

**Attach a concrete time estimate when the stretch is long.** Minutes or hours, based on a named earlier run, or marked as an assumption. An estimate informs and never gates. Outside that case, do not open a task with one nobody asked for, and never use one to argue against starting. When the task is too big for one pass, name the smallest slice worth delivering, start there and say what is deferred.

**A synthesis is not a file path.** When a long run produces findings, the message carries the answer, the few findings behind it, the uncertainty that matters and what was ruled out. The artifact path follows them. Per-iteration narration and internal metrics stay out. Where a run has its own completion contract, as the deep-loop modes do, that contract wins.

The failure this prevents: twenty minutes of silence, then a result the reader has to
reverse-engineer a plan from, or a path and a count handed over in place of a report.

---

## 9. WHEN THE READER DID NOT FOLLOW

"I don't follow", "what?" or "too abstract" calls for a change of modality: a concrete
example, a numbered sequence, a smaller first step or a picture using the runtime's visual
capability. Do not repeat the same explanation at greater length.

"say that more plainly", "in simple terms" or "rewrite that" calls for a plain
re-render: a copy edit that rewords without reordering, cutting or adding. Every claim,
number, caveat and conclusion keeps its strength. Code, commands, paths, identifiers,
quotations and numbers stay byte-exact. The wording standard is
[hvr-rules.md](../skills/sk-doc/sk-create-with-human-voice/references/hvr-rules.md). If a
plainer word would change what a sentence claims, keep the original.

The failure this prevents: the same explanation repeats, or the plain rewrite changes the claim.

---

## 10. WHAT THIS RULE IS NOT

- **Not a constraint on rigor.** Nothing here softens a claim, a caveat, or a verification standard owned by [`evidence-and-proof.md`](evidence-and-proof.md) or [`uncertainty-and-honesty.md`](uncertainty-and-honesty.md).
- **Not a voice to perform.** When honoring a rule here would weaken the answer, keep the answer.
- **Not a license to omit.** "Match length to the question" is about the reader's need,
  never about leaving out what they have to know.
- **Not licence to skip the analysis.** Verdict first is an ordering of the writing, never
  a shortcut in the reasoning.

---

## 11. SELF-CHECK

- [ ] The first line carries the answer or the action, not a label, an announcement or a setup, and the outcome is complete by the second line.
- [ ] I reached the verdict by analysis, recommended one path with its trade-off and stated the assumptions I made.
- [ ] Where the request was ambiguous or I delivered a different version of it, the first lines say which reading I took and why.
- [ ] Each paragraph stands alone and carries the reader forward, and multi-step work reads as a numbered list.
- [ ] No group runs past five items, nothing was dropped to keep it under, and a tangent appears once at the end or not at all.
- [ ] Every sentence carries information, each fact appears once, and no runtime instruction to plan privately was copied into the reply.
- [ ] Length matches what the reader asked, no table stands in a reply, and nothing was compressed past the point where the reader has to re-expand it.
- [ ] Every sentence carries one idea in plain words and says how it attaches to the one before.
- [ ] Arrows, bare IDs, counts and fragments were turned into sentences, and every path, hash or flag is one the reader will act on.
- [ ] No em dash, no semicolon, no serial comma.
- [ ] Before a long stretch I said where I was going, with a concrete time estimate when it was long, and no estimate argued against starting.
- [ ] A synthesis I reported carries its findings, not just its path and its counts.
- [ ] When the reader did not follow, I changed modality, and a plain re-render kept every claim, number and caveat at the same strength.

1:#!/usr/bin/env node
2:// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
3:// MODULE: Doctor Overlay Engine
4:// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
5:'use strict';
6:
7:// Keeps a project's own divergences from a vendored Skilled tree ("overlays")
8:// and puts them back after the tree is re-copied from upstream.
9://
10:// WHY this exists: a vendored tree is replaced wholesale whenever a new upstream
11:// release is copied in, and there is no update base that would carry local
12:// edits forward. Every divergence therefore lives twice: once as a canonical
13:// copy in the overlay folder, and once as the applied file in the tree. This
14:// engine keeps the two equal, records the bytes it last applied to each
15:// destination, and tells a live edit apart from an overlay edit.
16://
17:// The two rules the whole design rests on:
18://   1. A vendor file is never overwritten when upstream has changed it. The
19://      engine stops and names the path instead, because guessing which hunks to
20://      keep is how a silent regression starts.
21://   2. Applying twice writes nothing the second time.
22://
23:// Usage:
24://   node overlay-engine.cjs status [--root <tree>] [--json] [--audit]
25://     Read-only. One line per destination not in sync, then the full check.
26://     Exit 0 when everything is in sync, 1 when anything is not, 2 on error.
27://   node overlay-engine.cjs apply [--root <tree>]
28://     Apply overlay edits, regenerate, verify. Exit 0, 1 on conflict, 2 on error.
29://   node overlay-engine.cjs apply --capture <path> [--root <tree>]
30://     Save a live edit back into its overlay source. Exit 0, 1 when the path is
31://     not live-edited, 2 on refusal or error.
32://   node overlay-engine.cjs --guard <path> [--root <tree>]
33://     Exit 0 when the path is not owned by an overlay, 1 when it is, 2 on error.
34://     With "strictWrites": true in the manifest, also exit 1 for a write under
35://     a vendored root that no overlay owns and the generated list does not name.
36://   node overlay-engine.cjs apply --adopt <path> [--root <tree>]
37://     Add a manifest entry for a vendored path no overlay owns yet, then apply.
38://     Exit 0, 1 when the apply that follows is blocked, 2 on refusal or error.
39://   node overlay-engine.cjs pre-commit [--root <tree>]
40://     With strictWrites on, exit 1 when a staged change touches a vendored path
41://     no overlay owns. Exit 0 otherwise, 2 on error.
42://   node overlay-engine.cjs apply --relayout [--root <tree>]
43://     Move every overlay source kept off its layout path onto it, back up what
44://     it leaves behind, and point the manifest there. Exit 0, 2 on a refusal.
45://   node overlay-engine.cjs repin --upstream <dir> --to <commit> [--stage <dir>] [--root <tree>] [--json]
46://     Move the tree to a new upstream commit and apply the overlays again. With
47://     --stage, do it on a copy of the tree in <dir> and leave the tree alone.
48://     Exit 0 when clean, 1 when apply was blocked or the check failed (the run
49://     folder stays for --rollback), 2 on a refusal before any write or an error.
50://   node overlay-engine.cjs repin --rollback <run-id> [--root <tree>] [--json]
51://     Put back the bytes a re-pin run backed up. Exit 0, 2 on refusal or error.
52://
53:// --root defaults to the tree this script is vendored in.
54://
55:// Standard library only: this must run on a bare checkout with no install step.
56:
57:// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
58:// 1. IMPORTS

110:
111:/**
112: * Return the hexadecimal SHA-256 digest for a byte buffer.
113: * @param {Buffer} buffer Bytes to hash.
114: * @returns {string} The lowercase hexadecimal digest.
115: */
116:function sha256(buffer) {
117:  return crypto.createHash('sha256').update(buffer).digest('hex');
118:}
119:
120:function toPosix(filePath) {
121:  return filePath.split(path.sep).join('/');
122:}
123:
124:function readBufferOrNull(filePath) {
125:  try {
126:    return fs.readFileSync(filePath);
127:  } catch (error) {
128:    if (error && error.code === 'ENOENT') return null;
129:    throw error;
130:  }
131:}
132:
133:function pathExists(filePath) {
134:  try {
135:    fs.lstatSync(filePath);
136:    return true;
137:  } catch (error) {
138:    if (error && (error.code === 'ENOENT' || error.code === 'ENOTDIR')) return false;
139:    throw error;
140:  }
141:}
142:
143:function isFile(filePath) {
144:  try {
145:    return fs.lstatSync(filePath).isFile();
146:  } catch {
147:    return false;
148:  }
149:}
150:
151:function isDirectory(filePath) {
152:  try {
153:    return fs.lstatSync(filePath).isDirectory();
154:  } catch {
155:    return false;
156:  }
157:}
158:
159:function isWithin(basePath, candidatePath) {
160:  const relative = path.relative(basePath, candidatePath);
161:  return relative === '' || (!path.isAbsolute(relative) && relative !== '..' && !relative.startsWith('..' + path.sep));
162:}
163:
164:function relativeKey(root, filePath) {
165:  const absoluteRoot = path.resolve(root);
166:  const absoluteFile = path.resolve(filePath);
167:  if (!isWithin(absoluteRoot, absoluteFile) || absoluteFile === absoluteRoot) {
168:    throw new Error('destination escapes the sync root: ' + filePath);
169:  }
170:  return toPosix(path.relative(absoluteRoot, absoluteFile));
171:}
172:
173:/**
174: * Resolve one remove-root path, refusing any that leaves the tree.
175: * @param {string} root Destination tree root.
176: * @param {string} relativePath Path as the manifest names it.
177: * @returns {string} The absolute path to remove.
178: */
179:function resolveRemoveRootPath(root, relativePath) {
180:  if (typeof relativePath !== 'string' || relativePath.length === 0) {
181:    throw new Error('removal path must be a non-empty relative path');
182:  }
183:  if (relativePath.includes('\0')) throw new Error('removal path contains a null byte');
184:  if (path.isAbsolute(relativePath) || path.win32.isAbsolute(relativePath)) {
185:    throw new Error(`removal path must be relative: ${relativePath}`);
186:  }
187:  if (relativePath.includes('\\')) throw new Error(`removal path must use forward slashes: ${relativePath}`);
188:  if (relativePath.split('/').includes('..')) throw new Error(`removal path contains a '..' segment: ${relativePath}`);
189:
190:  const absoluteRoot = path.resolve(root);
191:  const absolutePath = path.resolve(absoluteRoot, relativePath);
192:  if (absolutePath === absoluteRoot || !isWithin(absoluteRoot, absolutePath)) {
193:    throw new Error(`removal path resolves outside the tree root: ${relativePath}`);
194:  }
195:
196:  // A symlinked parent is safe only when it resolves back inside the tree. The
197:  // leaf itself may be a symlink, because the plan deletes that link unopened.
198:  let realRoot;
199:  try {
200:    realRoot = fs.realpathSync.native(absoluteRoot);
201:  } catch (error) {
202:    if (!error || (error.code !== 'ENOENT' && error.code !== 'ENOTDIR')) throw error;
203:    realRoot = absoluteRoot;
204:  }
205:  let parentPath = absoluteRoot;
206:  for (const part of path.relative(absoluteRoot, absolutePath).split(path.sep).slice(0, -1)) {
207:    parentPath = path.join(parentPath, part);
208:    let stats;
209:    try {
210:      stats = fs.lstatSync(parentPath);
211:    } catch (error) {
212:      if (error && (error.code === 'ENOENT' || error.code === 'ENOTDIR')) break;
213:      throw error;
214:    }
215:    if (stats.isSymbolicLink()) {
216:      let resolvedParent;
217:      try {
218:        resolvedParent = fs.realpathSync.native(parentPath);
219:      } catch {
220:        throw new Error(`removal path has an unresolved symlink parent: ${relativePath}`);
221:      }
222:      if (!isWithin(realRoot, resolvedParent)) {
223:        throw new Error(`removal path traverses outside the tree through a symlink parent: ${relativePath}`);
224:      }
225:    }
226:    if (!stats.isDirectory()) break;
227:  }
228:  return absolutePath;
229:}
230:
231:/**
232: * List a path and everything below it, deepest entries first.
233: * @param {string} filePath Path to remove.
234: * @returns {string[]} Absolute paths, children before their folder.
235: */
236:function collectRemovalPaths(filePath) {
237:  let stats;
238:  try {
239:    stats = fs.lstatSync(filePath);
240:  } catch (error) {
241:    if (error && (error.code === 'ENOENT' || error.code === 'ENOTDIR')) return [];
242:    throw error;
243:  }
244:  if (!stats.isDirectory()) return [filePath];
245:
246:  const paths = [];
247:  for (const name of fs.readdirSync(filePath)) {
248:    paths.push(...collectRemovalPaths(path.join(filePath, name)));
249:  }
250:  paths.push(filePath);
251:  return paths;
252:}
253:
254:function writeFile(filePath, buffer, mode) {
255:  fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(filePath), { recursive: true });
256:  fs.writeFileSync(filePath, buffer, { mode: mode || 0o644 });
257:}
258:
259:function ensureSymlink(filePath, target) {
260:  fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(filePath), { recursive: true });
261:  try {
262:    fs.unlinkSync(filePath);
263:  } catch (error) {
264:    if (!error || error.code !== 'ENOENT') throw error;
265:  }
266:  fs.symlinkSync(target, filePath);
267:}
268:
269:function removePath(filePath) {
270:  let stats;
271:  try {
272:    stats = fs.lstatSync(filePath);
273:  } catch (error) {
274:    if (error && error.code === 'ENOENT') return false;
275:    throw error;
276:  }
277:  if (stats.isDirectory()) fs.rmSync(filePath, { recursive: true, force: true });
278:  else fs.unlinkSync(filePath);
279:  return true;
280:}
281:
282:// A vendor folder whose every file was removed would otherwise stay behind as an
283:// empty directory, which reads as "still part of the skill" to a human and to a
284:// directory listing in a check.
285:function pruneEmptyDirs(dir) {
286:  if (!isDirectory(dir)) return;
287:  for (const dirent of fs.readdirSync(dir, { withFileTypes: true })) {
288:    if (dirent.isDirectory()) pruneEmptyDirs(path.join(dir, dirent.name));
289:  }
290:  if (fs.readdirSync(dir).length === 0) fs.rmdirSync(dir);
291:}
292:
293:/**
294: * Every entry below `dir`, keyed by its path relative to `dir`. Symlinks are
295: * returned as links with their target string, never followed, because the
296: * vendored tree keeps its shape with relative links.
297: * @param {string} dir Folder to walk.
298: * @returns {Map<string, object>} Entries keyed by posix path relative to `dir`.
299: */
300:function walkTree(dir) {
301:  const entries = new Map();
302:  if (!isDirectory(dir)) return entries;
303:  const stack = [''];
304:  while (stack.length > 0) {
305:    const rel = stack.pop();
306:    const abs = rel ? path.join(dir, rel) : dir;
307:    for (const dirent of fs.readdirSync(abs, { withFileTypes: true })) {
308:      const childRel = rel ? path.posix.join(rel, dirent.name) : dirent.name;
309:      const childAbs = path.join(dir, childRel);
310:      const stats = fs.lstatSync(childAbs);
311:      if (stats.isSymbolicLink()) {
312:        entries.set(childRel, { kind: 'symlink', target: fs.readlinkSync(childAbs) });
313:      } else if (stats.isDirectory()) {
314:        stack.push(childRel);
315:      } else {
316:        entries.set(childRel, {
317:          kind: 'file',
318:          buffer: fs.readFileSync(childAbs),
319:          mode: stats.mode & 0o777,
320:        });
321:      }
322:    }
323:  }
324:  return entries;
325:}
326:
327:/**
328: * Absolute paths of every non-directory entry below a folder, symlinks included.
329: * @param {string} directoryPath Folder to list.
330: * @returns {string[]} Absolute paths.
331: */
332:function listTreePaths(directoryPath) {
333:  const paths = [];
334:  if (!isDirectory(directoryPath)) return paths;
335:  const pending = [directoryPath];
336:  while (pending.length > 0) {
337:    const current = pending.pop();
338:    for (const item of fs.readdirSync(current, { withFileTypes: true })) {
339:      const absolute = path.join(current, item.name);
340:      const stats = fs.lstatSync(absolute);
341:      if (stats.isDirectory()) pending.push(absolute);
342:      else paths.push(absolute);
343:    }
344:  }
345:  return paths;
346:}
347:
348:/**
349: * Resolve symlinks in the longest existing prefix of a path.
350: * @param {string} filePath Path that may not exist yet.
351: * @returns {string} The path with its existing part resolved.
352: */
353:function canonicalPath(filePath) {
354:  const absolute = path.resolve(filePath);
355:  const remaining = [];
356:  let current = absolute;
357:  while (true) {
358:    try {
359:      const resolved = fs.realpathSync.native(current);
360:      return path.join(resolved, ...remaining.reverse());
361:    } catch (error) {
362:      if (!error || (error.code !== 'ENOENT' && error.code !== 'ENOTDIR')) throw error;
363:      const parent = path.dirname(current);
364:      if (parent === current) return absolute;
365:      remaining.push(path.basename(current));
366:      current = parent;
367:    }
368:  }
369:}
370:
371:function canonicalPathWithoutLeaf(filePath) {
372:  const absolute = path.resolve(filePath);
373:  return path.join(canonicalPath(path.dirname(absolute)), path.basename(absolute));
374:}
375:
376:function describe(value) {
377:  return JSON.stringify(value);
378:}
379:
380:// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
381:// 4. OVERLAY FOLDER, MANIFEST AND STATE
382://
383:// The overlay folder is named by one setting file at the tree root. It cannot
384:// live inside `.skilled`, because a re-pin overwrites that folder, and it cannot
385:// live in the manifest, because the manifest sits inside the folder it would
386:// locate. Everything else in the engine asks the context built here.
387:// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
388:
389:/**
390: * Read the overlay folder from the tree's setting file.
391: * This is the only reader of the setting; every caller goes through it.
392: * @param {string} root Vendored tree root.
393: * @returns {string} The overlay folder, relative to the root, with forward slashes.
394: */
395:function resolveOverlayDir(root) {
396:  const settingPath = path.join(path.resolve(root), SETTING_FILE);
397:  const buffer = readBufferOrNull(settingPath);
398:  if (!buffer) return DEFAULT_OVERLAY_FOLDER;
399:  let parsed;
400:  try {
401:    parsed = JSON.parse(buffer.toString('utf8'));
402:  } catch (error) {
403:    throw new Error(`${SETTING_FILE} is not valid JSON: ${error.message}`);
404:  }
405:  const value = parsed && typeof parsed === 'object' ? parsed.overlayDir : undefined;
406:  if (typeof value !== 'string' || value.trim() === '') {
407:    throw new Error(`${SETTING_FILE} must name the overlay folder as a non-empty string`);
408:  }
409:  if (value.includes('\0') || value.includes('\\') || path.isAbsolute(value) || path.win32.isAbsolute(value)) {
410:    throw new Error(`${SETTING_FILE} must name a relative folder with forward slashes: ${value}`);
411:  }
412:  const normalized = path.posix.normalize(value).replace(/\/+$/, '');
413:  if (normalized === '.' || normalized === '' || normalized === '..' || normalized.startsWith('../')) {
414:    throw new Error(`${SETTING_FILE} names a folder outside the tree root: ${value}`);
415:  }
416:  return normalized;
417:}
418:
419:/**
420: * Build the paths one engine run works against.
421: * @param {string} root Vendored tree root.
422: * @returns {{root: string, overlayLabel: string, overlayRoot: string,
423: *   manifestPath: string, statePath: string, lockPath: string}}
424: */
425:function createContext(root) {
426:  const absoluteRoot = path.resolve(root);
427:  const overlayLabel = resolveOverlayDir(absoluteRoot);
428:  const overlayRoot = path.join(absoluteRoot, ...overlayLabel.split('/'));
429:  return {
430:    root: absoluteRoot,
431:    overlayLabel,
432:    overlayRoot,
433:    hasSetting: pathExists(path.join(absoluteRoot, SETTING_FILE)),
434:    manifestPath: path.join(overlayRoot, MANIFEST_FILE),
435:    statePath: path.join(overlayRoot, STATE_FILE),
436:    lockPath: path.join(overlayRoot, LOCK_FILE),
437:  };
438:}
439:
440:/**
441: * Load the overlay manifest from the overlay folder.
442: * @param {object} ctx Engine context.
443: * @returns {object} Parsed overlay manifest.
444: */
445:function loadManifest(ctx) {
446:  const buffer = readBufferOrNull(ctx.manifestPath);
447:  if (!buffer) {
448:    const where = toPosix(path.relative(ctx.root, ctx.manifestPath));
449:    // Without the setting file the folder is a guess, so the error says what was
450:    // guessed and how to name the real folder rather than blaming the manifest.
451:    if (!ctx.hasSetting) {
452:      throw new Error(`no overlay manifest at ${where}: with no ${SETTING_FILE} at the tree root the engine ` +
453:        `looks in ${DEFAULT_OVERLAY_FOLDER}/; if the overlays live elsewhere, add ` +
454:        `${SETTING_FILE} at the tree root naming that folder`);
455:    }
456:    throw new Error('no overlay manifest at ' + where);
457:  }
458:  const manifest = JSON.parse(buffer.toString('utf8'));
459:  if (!manifest || !Array.isArray(manifest.overlays)) throw new Error('overlay manifest has no overlays array');
460:  return manifest;
461:}
462:
463:function emptyState(pin) {
464:  return { schemaVersion: STATE_SCHEMA_VERSION, pin, files: {}, links: {}, items: {}, sets: {}, exists: false };
465:}
466:
467:/**
468: * Load the hashes of what each destination last received from its overlay, the
469: * layout link targets this engine itself put there, the array items it added and
470: * the values its `set` operations wrote.
471: * @param {object} ctx Engine context.
472: * @param {object} manifest Loaded overlay manifest.
473: * @returns {{schemaVersion: number, pin: string, files: Record<string, string>,
474: *   links: Record<string, string>, items: Record<string, object[]>,
475: *   sets: Record<string, object[]>, exists: boolean}}
476: */
477:function loadState(ctx, manifest) {
478:  const buffer = readBufferOrNull(ctx.statePath);
479:  if (!buffer) return emptyState(manifest.pin.commit);
480:  let parsed;
481:  try {
482:    parsed = JSON.parse(buffer.toString('utf8'));
483:  } catch (error) {
484:    throw new Error(STATE_FILE + ' is not valid JSON: ' + error.message);
485:  }
486:  if (parsed.schemaVersion !== STATE_SCHEMA_VERSION || typeof parsed.pin !== 'string' ||
487:      !parsed.files || typeof parsed.files !== 'object' || Array.isArray(parsed.files)) {
488:    throw new Error(STATE_FILE + ' has an unsupported shape');
489:  }
490:  // An older state file predates the link records, so a missing key means the
491:  // engine has no link it may retarget: every link on disk is someone else's.
492:  const links = parsed.links === undefined ? {} : parsed.links;
493:  if (!links || typeof links !== 'object' || Array.isArray(links) ||
494:      Object.values(links).some((target) => typeof target !== 'string')) {
495:    throw new Error(STATE_FILE + ' has an unsupported shape');
496:  }
497:  // A state file written before the added items were recorded says the engine
498:  // added nothing, so a missing key means no recorded item may ever be removed.
499:  const items = parsed.items === undefined ? {} : parsed.items;
500:  if (!items || typeof items !== 'object' || Array.isArray(items) ||
501:      Object.values(items).some((records) => !Array.isArray(records))) {
502:    throw new Error(STATE_FILE + ' has an unsupported shape');
503:  }
504:  // A state file written before a `set` was recorded says the engine wrote none,
505:  // so a missing key means no value may ever be returned to the pin's.
506:  const sets = parsed.sets === undefined ? {} : parsed.sets;
507:  if (!sets || typeof sets !== 'object' || Array.isArray(sets) ||
508:      Object.values(sets).some((records) => !Array.isArray(records))) {
509:    throw new Error(STATE_FILE + ' has an unsupported shape');
510:  }
511:  for (const [destination, hash] of Object.entries(parsed.files)) {
512:    if (path.isAbsolute(destination) || destination.includes('\\') ||
513:        destination.split('/').some((part) => part === '..' || part === '.' || part === '') ||
514:        !/^[a-f0-9]{64}$/i.test(hash)) {
515:      throw new Error(STATE_FILE + ' has an invalid file entry: ' + destination);
516:    }
517:  }
518:  return {
519:    schemaVersion: STATE_SCHEMA_VERSION,
520:    pin: parsed.pin,
521:    files: parsed.files,
522:    links,
523:    items,
524:    sets,
525:    exists: true,
526:  };
527:}
528:
529:/**
530: * Write the applied-hash state, sorted, only when its bytes would change.
531: * @param {object} ctx Engine context.
532: * @param {{pin: string, files: Record<string, string>, links?: Record<string, string>,
533: *   items?: Record<string, object[]>, sets?: Record<string, object[]>}} state
534: *   State to record.
535: * @returns {boolean} Whether the file was written.
536: */
537:function writeStateIfChanged(ctx, state) {
538:  const sortedFiles = {};
539:  for (const key of Object.keys(state.files).sort()) sortedFiles[key] = state.files[key];
540:  const sortedLinks = {};
541:  for (const key of Object.keys(state.links || {}).sort()) sortedLinks[key] = state.links[key];
542:  const sortedItems = {};
543:  for (const key of Object.keys(state.items || {}).sort()) sortedItems[key] = state.items[key];
544:  const sortedSets = {};
545:  for (const key of Object.keys(state.sets || {}).sort()) sortedSets[key] = state.sets[key];
546:  const body = {
547:    schemaVersion: STATE_SCHEMA_VERSION,
548:    pin: state.pin,
549:    files: sortedFiles,
550:  };
551:  // A tree with no layout links keeps the bytes it had before links were
552:  // recorded, so an overlay-only state file never churns on rewrite.
553:  if (Object.keys(sortedLinks).length > 0) body.links = sortedLinks;
554:  // The added items follow the same rule: a tree whose overlays add none keeps
555:  // the state file bytes it already had.
556:  if (Object.keys(sortedItems).length > 0) body.items = sortedItems;
557:  // So do the recorded sets: a tree whose overlays hold no `set` writes no key.
558:  if (Object.keys(sortedSets).length > 0) body.sets = sortedSets;
559:  const content = JSON.stringify(body, null, 2) + '\n';
560:  const current = readBufferOrNull(ctx.statePath);
561:  if (current && current.toString('utf8') === content) return false;
562:  fs.writeFileSync(ctx.statePath, content, 'utf8');
563:  return true;
564:}
565:
566:function sourceRelative(ctx, sourcePath) {
567:  const absolute = path.resolve(sourcePath);
568:  if (!isWithin(ctx.overlayRoot, absolute) || absolute === ctx.overlayRoot) {
569:    throw new Error('overlay source escapes ' + ctx.overlayLabel + ': ' + sourcePath);
570:  }
571:  return toPosix(path.relative(ctx.overlayRoot, absolute));
572:}
573:
574:// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
575:// 5. JSON SOURCE MAP
576://
577:// The vendored routing files are hand-formatted: some keep short string arrays
578:// on one line, others put one string per line. Parsing and re-serialising the
579:// whole document would rewrite every untouched line and turn a one-entry edit
580:// into a whole-file diff, so the edits are applied to the original text and
581:// only the touched range changes. This parser records the character span of
582:// every value and member; nothing else about the document is altered.
583:// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
584:
585:/**
586: * Parse JSON text into nodes that keep their character spans.
587: * @param {string} text Document text.
588: * @returns {object} Root node with `start`, `end` and children.
589: */
590:function parseJsonSource(text) {
591:  let index = 0;
592:  const length = text.length;
593:
594:  function fail(message) {
595:    throw new Error(`invalid JSON at offset ${index}: ${message}`);
596:  }
597:
598:  function skipWhitespace() {
599:    while (index < length) {
600:      const ch = text[index];
601:      if (ch === ' ' || ch === '\t' || ch === '\n' || ch === '\r') index += 1;
602:      else break;
603:    }
604:  }
605:
606:  function parseString() {
607:    const start = index;
608:    if (text[index] !== '"') fail('expected a string');
609:    index += 1;
610:    let raw = '';
611:    while (index < length) {
612:      const ch = text[index];
613:      if (ch === '\\') {
614:        raw += text.slice(index, index + 2);
615:        index += 2;
616:        continue;
617:      }
618:      if (ch === '"') {
619:        index += 1;
620:        return { value: JSON.parse(`"${raw}"`), start, end: index };
621:      }
622:      raw += ch;
623:      index += 1;
624:    }
625:    fail('unterminated string');
626:    return null;
627:  }
628:
629:  function parseValue() {
630:    skipWhitespace();
631:    const start = index;
632:    const ch = text[index];
633:    if (ch === '{') return parseObject();
634:    if (ch === '[') return parseArray();
635:    if (ch === '"') {
636:      const parsed = parseString();
637:      return { type: 'string', start, end: index, value: parsed.value };
638:    }
639:    if (text.startsWith('true', index)) {
640:      index += 4;
641:      return { type: 'boolean', start, end: index, value: true };
642:    }
643:    if (text.startsWith('false', index)) {
644:      index += 5;
645:      return { type: 'boolean', start, end: index, value: false };
646:    }
647:    if (text.startsWith('null', index)) {
648:      index += 4;
649:      return { type: 'null', start, end: index, value: null };
650:    }
651:    const match = /^-?(?:0|[1-9]\d*)(?:\.\d+)?(?:[eE][+-]?\d+)?/.exec(text.slice(index));
652:    if (!match) fail('expected a value');
653:    index += match[0].length;
654:    return { type: 'number', start, end: index, value: Number(match[0]) };
655:  }
656:
657:  function parseObject() {
658:    const start = index;
659:    index += 1;
660:    const members = [];
661:    skipWhitespace();
662:    if (text[index] === '}') {
663:      index += 1;
664:      return { type: 'object', start, end: index, members };
665:    }
666:    while (true) {
667:      skipWhitespace();
668:      const keyStart = index;
669:      const key = parseString().value;
670:      skipWhitespace();
671:      if (text[index] !== ':') fail('expected ":"');
672:      index += 1;
673:      const value = parseValue();
674:      members.push({ key, keyStart, value });
675:      skipWhitespace();
676:      if (text[index] === ',') {
677:        index += 1;
678:        continue;
679:      }
680:      if (text[index] === '}') {
681:        index += 1;
682:        break;
683:      }
684:      fail('expected "," or "}"');
685:    }
686:    return { type: 'object', start, end: index, members };
687:  }
688:
689:  function parseArray() {
690:    const start = index;
691:    index += 1;
692:    const items = [];
693:    skipWhitespace();
694:    if (text[index] === ']') {
695:      index += 1;
696:      return { type: 'array', start, end: index, items };
697:    }
698:    while (true) {
699:      const value = parseValue();
700:      items.push(value);
701:      skipWhitespace();
702:      if (text[index] === ',') {
703:        index += 1;
704:        continue;
705:      }
706:      if (text[index] === ']') {
707:        index += 1;
708:        break;
709:      }
710:      fail('expected "," or "]"');
711:    }
712:    return { type: 'array', start, end: index, items };
713:  }
714:
715:  const root = parseValue();
716:  skipWhitespace();
717:  if (index !== length) fail('trailing content after the document');
718:  return root;
719:}
720:
721:/**
722: * Find the node at a key path in a parsed document.
723: * @param {object} root Root node from parseJsonSource.
724: * @param {Array<string|number>} keys Object keys and array indexes.
725: * @returns {object} The node at that path.
726: */
727:function nodeAtPath(root, keys) {
728:  let node = root;
729:  for (const key of keys) {
730:    if (node.type === 'object') {
731:      const member = node.members.find((entry) => entry.key === key);
732:      if (!member) throw new Error(`missing key "${keys.join('.')}"`);
733:      node = member.value;
734:    } else if (node.type === 'array') {
735:      const item = node.items[key];
736:      if (!item) throw new Error(`missing index ${key} on "${keys.join('.')}"`);
737:      node = item;
738:    } else {
739:      throw new Error(`cannot descend into a ${node.type} at "${keys.join('.')}"`);
740:    }
741:  }
742:  return node;
743:}
744:
745:function indentBefore(text, offset) {
746:  const lineStart = text.lastIndexOf('\n', offset - 1) + 1;
747:  let cursor = lineStart;
748:  while (cursor < offset && (text[cursor] === ' ' || text[cursor] === '\t')) cursor += 1;
749:  return cursor === offset ? text.slice(lineStart, cursor) : null;
750:}
751:
752:function isPrimitive(value) {
753:  return value === null || typeof value !== 'object';
754:}
755:
756:/**
757: * Serialises a value the way the surrounding document is written: objects and
758: * object arrays on their own lines, and short string arrays either inline or
759: * expanded according to the document's own habit.
760: * @param {*} value Value to serialise.
761: * @param {string} indent Indent of the line the value starts on.
762: * @param {{primitiveArrays?: string}} style Entry-set style.
763: * @returns {string} The serialised text.
764: */
765:function serializeValue(value, indent, style) {
766:  if (isPrimitive(value)) return JSON.stringify(value);
767:  const inner = indent + '  ';
768:  if (Array.isArray(value)) {
769:    if (value.length === 0) return '[]';
770:    const isInline = style.primitiveArrays !== 'multiline' && value.every(isPrimitive);
771:    if (isInline) return `[${value.map((item) => JSON.stringify(item)).join(', ')}]`;
772:    return `[\n${value.map((item) => inner + serializeValue(item, inner, style)).join(',\n')}\n${indent}]`;
773:  }
774:  const keys = Object.keys(value);
775:  if (keys.length === 0) return '{}';
776:  const members = keys.map((key) => `${inner}${JSON.stringify(key)}: ${serializeValue(value[key], inner, style)}`);
777:  return `{\n${members.join(',\n')}\n${indent}}`;
778:}
779:
780:function deepEqual(left, right) {
781:  if (isPrimitive(left) || isPrimitive(right)) return left === right;
782:  if (Array.isArray(left) !== Array.isArray(right)) return false;
783:  if (Array.isArray(left)) {
784:    if (left.length !== right.length) return false;
785:    return left.every((item, index) => deepEqual(item, right[index]));
786:  }
787:  const leftKeys = Object.keys(left).sort();
788:  const rightKeys = Object.keys(right).sort();
789:  if (leftKeys.length !== rightKeys.length) return false;
790:  if (!leftKeys.every((key, index) => key === rightKeys[index])) return false;
791:  return leftKeys.every((key) => deepEqual(left[key], right[key]));
792:}
793:
794:function findMember(node, key) {
795:  return node.members.find((member) => member.key === key);
796:}
797:
798:function matchIndex(arrayNode, match, identityKey) {
799:  const wanted = match[identityKey];
800:  const found = [];
801:  arrayNode.items.forEach((item, index) => {
802:    if (item.type !== 'object') return;
803:    const member = findMember(item, identityKey);
804:    if (member && member.value.value === wanted) found.push(index);
805:  });
806:  if (found.length > 1) throw new Error(`anchor "${wanted}" appears ${found.length} times`);
807:  return found.length === 1 ? found[0] : -1;
808:}
809:
810:/**
811: * The value that names a recorded item: the identity key's value for an object,
812: * the value itself for a primitive.
813: * @param {object} record One recorded added item.
814: * @returns {*} The item's identity.
815: */
816:function recordIdentity(record) {
817:  const identityKey = record.identityKey === undefined ? null : record.identityKey;
818:  if (identityKey === null || isPrimitive(record.item)) return record.item;
819:  return record.item[identityKey];
820:}
821:
822:/**
823: * Whether a recorded item and an item an entry set lists are the same array
824: * entry, so a re-apply recognises its own work and never an upstream item.
825: * @param {object} record One recorded added item.
826: * @param {Array<string|number>} path Array the entry set writes.
827: * @param {string} [identityKey] Key naming an object item; absent when the value is the identity.
828: * @param {*} item Item the entry set lists.
829: * @returns {boolean} Whether the two describe the same entry.
830: */
831:function isRecordedItem(record, path, identityKey, item) {
832:  if (!record || !deepEqual(record.path, path)) return false;
833:  const recordedKey = record.identityKey === undefined ? null : record.identityKey;
834:  const key = identityKey === undefined ? null : identityKey;
835:  if (recordedKey !== key) return false;
836:  if (key === null) return deepEqual(record.item, item);
837:  if (isPrimitive(record.item) || isPrimitive(item)) return false;
838:  return record.item[key] === item[key];
839:}
840:
841:/**
842: * Applies one operation to the parsed document and records the text edit that
843: * realises it. Nothing is written here; the caller decides whether to keep it.
844: * @param {object} operation One entry-set operation.
845: * @param {object} root Root node of the parsed document.
846: * @param {string} text Document text the nodes point into.
847: * @param {object} style Entry-set style.
848: * @param {object[]} edits Collects the text edits.
849: * @param {{canReplaceExisting: boolean, recordedItems?: object[],
850: *   onListed?: function, recordedSets?: object[], onSet?: function}} options
851: *   Whether a differing entry may be rewritten, the items this overlay already
852: *   added to the array, where each listed item is reported so a later pass can
853: *   recognise it as the engine's own work, the sets this overlay applied last
854: *   time, and where each `set` is reported so a later pass can take it back.
855: * @returns {string} 'changed' or 'unchanged'.
856: */
857:function planOperation(operation, root, text, style, edits, options) {
858:  if (operation.op === 'set') {
859:    const parentPath = operation.path.slice(0, -1);
860:    const lastKey = operation.path[operation.path.length - 1];
861:    const parent = operation.path.length > 0 ? nodeAtPath(root, parentPath) : null;
862:    // Every set is reported, changed or not, so a value an older engine wrote is
863:    // recorded by the first apply that still names it. A key stays marked as
864:    // added for as long as the record says this overlay put it there.
865:    const reportSet = (added) => {
866:      if (!options.onSet) return;
867:      options.onSet({ path: [...operation.path], value: operation.value, added });
868:    };
869:    const wasAdded = (options.recordedSets || [])
870:      .some((record) => record.added === true && deepEqual(record.path, operation.path));
871:    // A key the object does not hold yet is added after its last member, so one
872:    // entry set fits the file before and after upstream starts shipping the key.
873:    if (parent && parent.type === 'object' && typeof lastKey === 'string' && !findMember(parent, lastKey)) {
874:      const reference = parent.members[parent.members.length - 1];
875:      if (!reference) throw new Error(`cannot insert into the empty object at ${parentPath.join('.')}`);
876:      const indent = indentBefore(text, reference.keyStart) || '';
877:      edits.push({
878:        start: reference.value.end,
879:        end: reference.value.end,
880:        text: `,\n${indent}${JSON.stringify(lastKey)}: ${serializeValue(operation.value, indent, style)}`,
881:      });
882:      reportSet(true);
883:      return 'changed';
884:    }
885:    const target = nodeAtPath(root, operation.path);
886:    const current = JSON.parse(text.slice(target.start, target.end));
887:    reportSet(wasAdded);
888:    if (deepEqual(current, operation.value)) return 'unchanged';
889:    const indent = indentBefore(text, target.start);
890:    edits.push({
891:      start: target.start,
892:      end: target.end,
893:      text: serializeValue(operation.value, indent === null ? '' : indent, style),
894:    });
895:    return 'changed';
896:  }
897:
898:  if (operation.op === 'setKeyOnItem') {
899:    const arrayNode = nodeAtPath(root, operation.path);
900:    const index = matchIndex(arrayNode, operation.match, operation.identityKey);
901:    if (index < 0) throw new Error(`anchor ${describe(operation.match)} not found under ${operation.path.join('.')}`);
902:    const item = arrayNode.items[index];
903:    const member = findMember(item, operation.key);
904:    if (member) {
905:      const current = JSON.parse(text.slice(member.value.start, member.value.end));
906:      if (deepEqual(current, operation.value)) return 'unchanged';
907:      const indent = indentBefore(text, member.value.start);
908:      edits.push({
909:        start: member.value.start,
910:        end: member.value.end,
911:        text: serializeValue(operation.value, indent === null ? '' : indent, style),
912:      });
913:      return 'changed';
914:    }
915:    const last = item.members[item.members.length - 1];
916:    const indent = indentBefore(text, last.keyStart) || '';
917:    edits.push({
918:      start: last.value.end,
919:      end: last.value.end,
920:      text: `,\n${indent}${JSON.stringify(operation.key)}: ${serializeValue(operation.value, indent, style)}`,
921:    });
922:    return 'changed';
923:  }
924:
925:  if (operation.op === 'insertItems' || operation.op === 'appendItems') {
926:    const arrayNode = nodeAtPath(root, operation.path);
927:    const identityKey = operation.identityKey;
928:    const recordedItems = options.recordedItems || [];
929:    // Only an item this overlay put there is reported back as its own: an item
930:    // the live file already held and the state never recorded may be upstream's.
931:    const reportListed = (item) => {
932:      if (!options.onListed) return;
933:      options.onListed({
934:        path: [...operation.path],
935:        identityKey: identityKey === undefined ? null : identityKey,
936:        item,
937:      });
938:    };
939:    const wasRecorded = (item) => recordedItems
940:      .some((record) => isRecordedItem(record, operation.path, identityKey, item));
941:    const pending = [];
942:    let hasChanged = false;
943:    for (const item of operation.items) {
944:      if (!identityKey) {
945:        // A scalar list carries its own identity, so an existing value counts as
946:        // already applied. Without this, a second apply would append a duplicate.
947:        if (isPrimitive(item) && arrayNode.items.some((node) => node.value === item)) {
948:          if (wasRecorded(item)) reportListed(item);
949:          continue;
950:        }
951:        if (!isPrimitive(item)) throw new Error('an item list without an identity key cannot hold objects');
952:      }
953:      if (identityKey) {
954:        const existing = matchIndex(arrayNode, item, identityKey);
955:        if (existing >= 0) {
956:          const node = arrayNode.items[existing];
957:          const parsed = JSON.parse(text.slice(node.start, node.end));
958:          if (!deepEqual(parsed, item)) {
959:            // A differing entry is rewritten only when the live file still holds the
960:            // bytes this overlay last wrote: the entry set is then the one thing that
961:            // changed, so replacing the entry in place keeps every other line of the
962:            // document as it was. Any other difference may be someone's hand edit.
963:            if (!options.canReplaceExisting) {
964:              throw new Error(`existing entry ${describe(item[identityKey])} differs from the canonical copy`);
965:            }
966:            const indent = indentBefore(text, node.start);
967:            edits.push({
968:              start: node.start,
969:              end: node.end,
970:              text: serializeValue(item, indent === null ? '' : indent, style),
971:            });
972:            hasChanged = true;
973:          }
974:          if (wasRecorded(item)) reportListed(item);
975:          continue;
976:        }
977:      }
978:      pending.push(item);
979:      reportListed(item);
980:      hasChanged = true;
981:    }
982:    if (!hasChanged) return 'unchanged';
983:    // A pass that only rewrote existing entries has nothing left to append.
984:    if (pending.length === 0) return 'changed';
985:    const anchor = operation.before && identityKey ? matchIndex(arrayNode, operation.before, identityKey) : -1;
986:    const reference = anchor >= 0 ? arrayNode.items[anchor] : arrayNode.items[arrayNode.items.length - 1];
987:    if (!reference) throw new Error(`cannot append to the empty array at ${operation.path.join('.')}`);
988:    const indent = indentBefore(text, reference.start) || '';
989:    if (anchor >= 0) {
990:      const block = pending.map((item) => `${serializeValue(item, indent, style)},\n${indent}`).join('');
991:      edits.push({ start: reference.start, end: reference.start, text: block });
992:    } else {
993:      const block = pending.map((item) => `,\n${indent}${serializeValue(item, indent, style)}`).join('');
994:      edits.push({ start: reference.end, end: reference.end, text: block });
995:    }
996:    return 'changed';
997:  }
998:
999:  if (operation.op === 'insertObjectKeys') {
1000:    const objectNode = nodeAtPath(root, operation.path);
1001:    const pending = [];
1002:    let hasRewritten = false;
1003:    for (const entry of operation.entries) {
1004:      const existing = findMember(objectNode, entry.key);
1005:      if (existing) {
1006:        const parsed = JSON.parse(text.slice(existing.value.start, existing.value.end));
1007:        if (!deepEqual(parsed, entry.value)) {
1008:          // Same rule as a keyed array item: rewrite the value in place only while the
1009:          // live file still holds this overlay's last applied bytes.
1010:          if (!options.canReplaceExisting) {
1011:            throw new Error(`existing entry "${entry.key}" differs from the canonical copy`);
1012:          }
1013:          const indent = indentBefore(text, existing.keyStart);
1014:          edits.push({
1015:            start: existing.value.start,
1016:            end: existing.value.end,
1017:            text: serializeValue(entry.value, indent === null ? '' : indent, style),
1018:          });
1019:          hasRewritten = true;
1020:        }
1021:        continue;
1022:      }
1023:      pending.push(entry);
1024:    }
1025:    if (pending.length === 0) return hasRewritten ? 'changed' : 'unchanged';
1026:    const anchor = operation.beforeKey ? findMember(objectNode, operation.beforeKey) : null;
1027:    const reference = anchor || objectNode.members[objectNode.members.length - 1];
1028:    if (!reference) throw new Error(`cannot insert into the empty object at ${operation.path.join('.')}`);
1029:    const indent = indentBefore(text, reference.keyStart) || '';
1030:    const render = (entry) => `${JSON.stringify(entry.key)}: ${serializeValue(entry.value, indent, style)}`;
1031:    if (anchor) {
1032:      edits.push({
1033:        start: reference.keyStart,
1034:        end: reference.keyStart,
1035:        text: `${pending.map((entry) => `${render(entry)},\n${indent}`).join('')}`,
1036:      });
1037:    } else {
1038:      edits.push({
1039:        start: reference.value.end,
1040:        end: reference.value.end,
1041:        text: pending.map((entry) => `,\n${indent}${render(entry)}`).join(''),
1042:      });
1043:    }
1044:    return 'changed';
1045:  }
1046:
1047:  throw new Error(`unknown operation "${operation.op}"`);
1048:}
1049:
1050:function applyEdits(text, edits) {
1051:  const ordered = [...edits].sort((left, right) => right.start - left.start);
1052:  let output = text;
1053:  for (const edit of ordered) {
1054:    output = output.slice(0, edit.start) + edit.text + output.slice(edit.end);
1055:  }
1056:  return output;
1057:}
1058:
1059:/**
1060: * Fold each later setKeyOnItem into the item an earlier insert in the same set
1061: * adds. Without this, a second apply compares the live item, which already
1062: * carries the later key, with the bare inserted item and reads a hand edit.
1063: * @param {object[]} operations Operations of one entry set, in order.
1064: * @returns {object[]} Operations whose inserted items carry their final keys.
1065: */
1066:function withLaterKeysMerged(operations) {
1067:  return operations.map((operation, index) => {
1068:    if ((operation.op !== 'insertItems' && operation.op !== 'appendItems') || !operation.identityKey) return operation;
1069:    const identityKey = operation.identityKey;
1070:    const items = operation.items.map((item) => {
1071:      let merged = item;
1072:      for (const later of operations.slice(index + 1)) {
1073:        if (later.op !== 'setKeyOnItem' || later.identityKey !== identityKey) continue;
1074:        if (!deepEqual(later.path, operation.path) || later.match[identityKey] !== item[identityKey]) continue;
1075:        merged = { ...merged, [later.key]: later.value };
1076:      }
1077:      return merged;
1078:    });
1079:    return { ...operation, items };
1080:  });
1081:}
1082:
1083:/**
1084: * Read the array a recorded item was added to.
1085: * @param {object} root Root node of a parsed document.
1086: * @param {Array<string|number>} path Array path the item was added at.
1087: * @returns {object|null} The array node, or null when the path holds no array now.
1088: */
1089:function recordedArrayAt(root, path) {
1090:  let node;
1091:  try {
1092:    node = nodeAtPath(root, path);
1093:  } catch (error) {
1094:    return null;
1095:  }
1096:  return node.type === 'array' ? node : null;
1097:}
1098:
1099:/**
1100: * Find the live entry that carries a recorded item's identity, so an item whose
1101: * identity was changed by hand is left alone rather than deleted by mistake.
1102: * @param {object} arrayNode Parsed array node.
1103: * @param {object} record One recorded added item.
1104: * @returns {number} Index of the entry, or -1 when the array does not hold it.
1105: */
1106:function findRecordedIndex(arrayNode, record) {
1107:  const identityKey = record.identityKey === undefined ? null : record.identityKey;
1108:  const wanted = recordIdentity(record);
1109:  return arrayNode.items.findIndex((node) => {
1110:    if (identityKey !== null) {
1111:      if (node.type !== 'object') return false;
1112:      const member = findMember(node, identityKey);
1113:      return Boolean(member) && member.value.value === wanted;
1114:    }
1115:    if (node.type === 'object' || node.type === 'array') return false;
1116:    return node.value === wanted;
1117:  });
1118:}
1119:
1120:/**
1121: * The edit that deletes one array item together with the comma that separated
1122: * it, so the document stays valid JSON and keeps no dangling separator.
1123: * @param {object[]} items Parsed items of the array.
1124: * @param {number} index Item to delete.
1125: * @returns {{start: number, end: number, text: string}} The text edit.
1126: */
1127:function removalEdit(items, index) {
1128:  if (index > 0) return { start: items[index - 1].end, end: items[index].end, text: '' };
1129:  if (items.length > 1) return { start: items[index].start, end: items[1].start, text: '' };
1130:  return { start: items[index].start, end: items[index].end, text: '' };
1131:}
1132:
1133:/**
1134: * Whether an entry set still lists a recorded item.
1135: * @param {object} operation One entry-set operation.
1136: * @param {object} record One recorded added item.
1137: * @returns {boolean} Whether the operation re-lists the item.
1138: */
1139:function listsRecordedItem(operation, record) {
1140:  if (operation.op !== 'insertItems' && operation.op !== 'appendItems') return false;
1141:  return operation.items.some((item) => isRecordedItem(record, operation.path, operation.identityKey, item));
1142:}
1143:
1144:/**
1145: * Whether the pinned upstream copy already carries a recorded item at the same
1146: * path, which makes retracting that item a deletion of upstream's own line
1147: * rather than of this overlay's addition.
1148: * @param {string|null} upstreamText The destination at the pin, or null when no
1149: *   upstream copy could be read.
1150: * @param {object} record One recorded added item.
1151: * @returns {boolean} Whether upstream's array at that path holds the same item.
1152: */
1153:function upstreamShipsRecordedItem(upstreamText, record) {
1154:  if (upstreamText === null) return false;
1155:  let arrayNode;
1156:  try {
1157:    arrayNode = recordedArrayAt(parseJsonSource(upstreamText), record.path);
1158:  } catch (error) {
1159:    // An upstream copy that does not parse says nothing about the item, so the
1160:    // record is retracted as it always was.
1161:    return false;
1162:  }
1163:  if (!arrayNode) return false;
1164:  const index = findRecordedIndex(arrayNode, record);
1165:  if (index < 0) return false;
1166:  const node = arrayNode.items[index];
1167:  return deepEqual(JSON.parse(upstreamText.slice(node.start, node.end)), record.item);
1168:}
1169:
1170:/**
1171: * Delete every item this overlay added that its entry set no longer lists, so a
1172: * dropped entry leaves the live file instead of staying behind as a line the
1173: * entry set never mentions. An item that changed after it was applied is
1174: * refused rather than deleted: that edit belongs to a person, and the entry set
1175: * no longer describes what the item should be. An item upstream now ships is
1176: * left in place and only its record dropped, because the live line is then
1177: * upstream's and deleting it would remove upstream's own entry.
1178: * @param {string} text Live document text.
1179: * @param {object[]} operations Operations of one entry set, in order.
1180: * @param {object[]} recordedItems Items this overlay added to the destination.
1181: * @param {() => (string|null)} [readUpstreamText] Reads the destination as the
1182: *   pin holds it, or null when there is no upstream copy to read.
1183: * @returns {string} The text with every dropped item gone.
1184: */
1185:function removeDroppedItems(text, operations, recordedItems, readUpstreamText = () => null) {
1186:  let output = text;
1187:  for (const record of recordedItems) {
1188:    if (operations.some((operation) => listsRecordedItem(operation, record))) continue;
1189:    // The array, or the item others kept in it, may be gone already: either way
1190:    // nothing is left for this record to retract.
1191:    const arrayNode = recordedArrayAt(parseJsonSource(output), record.path);
1192:    if (!arrayNode) continue;
1193:    const index = findRecordedIndex(arrayNode, record);
1194:    if (index < 0) continue;
1195:    const node = arrayNode.items[index];
1196:    const live = JSON.parse(output.slice(node.start, node.end));
1197:    if (!deepEqual(live, record.item)) {
1198:      throw new Error('dropped entry ' + describe(recordIdentity(record)) +
1199:        ' was edited after it was applied; remove it by hand or restore it in the entry set');
1200:    }
1201:    if (upstreamShipsRecordedItem(readUpstreamText(), record)) continue;
1202:    output = applyEdits(output, [removalEdit(arrayNode.items, index)]);
1203:  }
1204:  return output;
1205:}
1206:
1207:/**
1208: * Read the node a recorded set wrote to.
1209: * @param {object} root Root node of a parsed document.
1210: * @param {Array<string|number>} path Path the set wrote.
1211: * @returns {object|null} The node, or null when the document holds nothing there.
1212: */
1213:function recordedNodeAt(root, path) {
1214:  try {
1215:    return nodeAtPath(root, path);
1216:  } catch (error) {
1217:    return null;
1218:  }
1219:}
1220:
1221:/**
1222: * The edit that deletes one object member together with the comma that
1223: * separated it, so the document keeps no dangling separator.
1224: * @param {object[]} members Parsed members of the object.
1225: * @param {number} index Member to delete.
1226: * @returns {{start: number, end: number, text: string}} The text edit.
1227: */
1228:function memberRemovalEdit(members, index) {
1229:  if (index > 0) return { start: members[index - 1].value.end, end: members[index].value.end, text: '' };
1230:  if (members.length > 1) return { start: members[index].keyStart, end: members[1].keyStart, text: '' };
1231:  return { start: members[index].keyStart, end: members[index].value.end, text: '' };
1232:}
1233:
1234:/**
1235: * The outermost array a recorded path goes through in one document. A path
1236: * step is an index when the node it steps into is an array, however the entry
1237: * set spelled the step, so the document decides and not the step's type.
1238: * @param {object} root Root node of a parsed document.
1239: * @param {Array<string|number>} path Path the set wrote.
1240: * @returns {{node: object, path: Array<string|number>}|null} The array node
1241: *   and the path that reaches it, or null when the path meets no array.
1242: */
1243:function arrayOnPath(root, path) {
1244:  for (let depth = 0; depth < path.length; depth += 1) {
1245:    const container = recordedNodeAt(root, path.slice(0, depth));
1246:    if (!container) return null;
1247:    if (container.type === 'array') return { node: container, path: path.slice(0, depth) };
1248:  }
1249:  return null;
1250:}
1251:
1252:/**
1253: * Whether two paths name one location. A step into an array may be spelled as
1254: * a number or as a string, and both spellings reach the same item.
1255: * @param {Array<string|number>} left One path.
1256: * @param {Array<string|number>} right The other path.
1257: * @returns {boolean} Whether the steps read the same once each is a string.
1258: */
1259:function samePath(left, right) {
1260:  return left.length === right.length && left.every((step, index) => String(step) === String(right[index]));
1261:}
1262:
1263:/**
1264: * Whether a path sits strictly inside another one.
1265: * @param {Array<string|number>} inner The path that may sit inside.
1266: * @param {Array<string|number>} outer The path that may hold it.
1267: * @returns {boolean} Whether `inner` starts with every step of `outer` and goes further.
1268: */
1269:function isInsidePath(inner, outer) {
1270:  return inner.length > outer.length && samePath(inner.slice(0, outer.length), outer);
1271:}
1272:
1273:/**
1274: * A copy of a parsed value with one location changed. A location the value does
1275: * not reach is left alone, so a missing step never invents structure.
1276: * @param {*} value Parsed JSON value.
1277: * @param {Array<string|number>} steps Location inside the value.
1278: * @param {(container: object, step: string|number) => void} change Edits the
1279: *   container that holds the location.
1280: * @returns {*} The changed copy.
1281: */
1282:function changedAt(value, steps, change) {
1283:  const copy = value === undefined ? undefined : JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(value));
1284:  let container = copy;
1285:  for (const step of steps.slice(0, -1)) {
1286:    if (container === null || typeof container !== 'object' || !(step in container)) return copy;
1287:    container = container[step];
1288:  }
1289:  if (container === null || typeof container !== 'object') return copy;
1290:  change(container, steps[steps.length - 1]);
1291:  return copy;
1292:}
1293:
1294:/**
1295: * A copy of a parsed value with every listed location blanked out, so two
1296: * values can be compared on what is left. A location at the value itself
1297: * blanks the whole value.
1298: * @param {*} value Parsed JSON value.
1299: * @param {Array<Array<string|number>>} locations Locations inside the value.
1300: * @returns {*} The masked copy.
1301: */
1302:function maskedValue(value, locations) {
1303:  if (locations.some((steps) => steps.length === 0)) return null;
1304:  return locations.reduce((masked, steps) => changedAt(masked, steps, (container, step) => {
1305:    if (!Array.isArray(container)) delete container[step];
1306:    else if (step in container) container[step] = null;
1307:  }), value);
1308:}
1309:
1310:/**
1311: * Keep one record per path, the last one written. A state file from an engine
1312: * that recorded every `set` can hold two for one path, and only the last value
1313: * is the one the live file carries.
1314: * @param {object[]} records Recorded sets of one destination.
1315: * @returns {object[]} The records with earlier duplicates of a path left out.
1316: */
1317:function lastRecordPerPath(records) {
1318:  return records.filter((record, index) =>
1319:    !records.slice(index + 1).some((later) => samePath(later.path, record.path)));
1320:}
1321:
1322:/**
1323: * Whether every array index on a recorded path names the same item in the live
1324: * file and in the pinned copy. An index is a position, so it is trusted only
1325: * while the two arrays hold the same number of items, this overlay neither
1326: * added an item to that array nor is about to, and the item at that index is
1327: * the pin's item apart from the values this overlay's operations wrote into it.
1328: * Equal lengths alone miss a file left on an older base after upstream swapped
1329: * or replaced an item. A set that wrote the whole item leaves nothing of it to
1330: * recognize, so then every other item of the array must be the pin's.
1331: * @param {{text: string, root: object}} liveDocument The live text and its root node.
1332: * @param {{text: string, root: object}} pinnedDocument The pinned text and its root node.
1333: * @param {Array<string|number>} path Path the set wrote.
1334: * @param {object[]} operations Operations of one entry set, in order.
1335: * @param {object[]} recordedSets Sets this overlay applied to the destination.
1336: * @param {object[]} recordedItems Items this overlay added to the destination.
1337: * @returns {boolean} Whether the path's indexes can be read against the pin.
1338: */
1339:function indexesMatchPin(liveDocument, pinnedDocument, path, operations, recordedSets, recordedItems) {
1340:  const writtenPaths = [
1341:    ...recordedSets.map((record) => record.path),
1342:    ...operations.filter((operation) => operation.op === 'set').map((operation) => operation.path),
1343:  ];
1344:  const readItem = (document, item) => JSON.parse(document.text.slice(item.start, item.end));
1345:  // Every location inside one item that this overlay writes, relative to the item.
1346:  const writtenInside = (itemPath) => {
1347:    const inside = (written) => samePath(written, itemPath) || isInsidePath(written, itemPath);
1348:    const relative = (written) => written.slice(itemPath.length);
1349:    const locations = writtenPaths.filter(inside).map(relative);
1350:    // A set on a path above the item wrote the whole item.
1351:    if (writtenPaths.some((written) => isInsidePath(itemPath, written))) locations.push([]);
1352:    for (const operation of operations) {
1353:      if (operation.op === 'setKeyOnItem' && samePath(operation.path, itemPath.slice(0, -1))) {
1354:        locations.push([operation.key]);
1355:      } else if (operation.op === 'insertObjectKeys' && inside(operation.path)) {
1356:        for (const entry of operation.entries || []) locations.push([...relative(operation.path), entry.key]);
1357:      } else if ((operation.op === 'insertItems' || operation.op === 'appendItems') && isInsidePath(operation.path, itemPath)) {
1358:        locations.push(relative(operation.path));
1359:      }
1360:    }
1361:    for (const item of recordedItems) if (item && isInsidePath(item.path, itemPath)) locations.push(relative(item.path));
1362:    return locations;
1363:  };
1364:  for (let depth = 0; depth < path.length; depth += 1) {
1365:    const container = path.slice(0, depth);
1366:    const live = recordedNodeAt(liveDocument.root, container);
1367:    const pinned = recordedNodeAt(pinnedDocument.root, container);
1368:    const liveIsArray = Boolean(live) && live.type === 'array';
1369:    const pinnedIsArray = Boolean(pinned) && pinned.type === 'array';
1370:    if (!liveIsArray && !pinnedIsArray) continue;
1371:    if (!liveIsArray || !pinnedIsArray) return false;
1372:    if (live.items.length !== pinned.items.length) return false;
1373:    if (recordedItems.some((item) => item && samePath(item.path, container))) return false;
1374:    const addsItems = operations.some((operation) =>
1375:      (operation.op === 'insertItems' || operation.op === 'appendItems') && samePath(operation.path, container));
1376:    if (addsItems) return false;
1377:    const maskedItems = (index) => {
1378:      const written = writtenInside([...container, index]);
1379:      return [
1380:        maskedValue(readItem(liveDocument, live.items[index]), written),
1381:        maskedValue(readItem(pinnedDocument, pinned.items[index]), written),
1382:      ];
1383:    };
1384:    const itemMatches = (index) => deepEqual(...maskedItems(index));
1385:    const index = Number(path[depth]);
1386:    if (!live.items[index] || !pinned.items[index]) return false;
1387:    if (!itemMatches(index)) return false;
1388:    // Masks that leave nothing of either item mean the overlay wrote all of it,
1389:    // whether in one set or in several, so the item itself proves nothing.
1390:    const nothingLeft = (value) => value === null ||
1391:      (typeof value === 'object' && !Array.isArray(value) && Object.keys(value).length === 0);
1392:    const wholeItemWritten = maskedItems(index).every(nothingLeft);
1393:    if (wholeItemWritten && !live.items.every((item, other) => other === index || itemMatches(other))) return false;
1394:  }
1395:  return true;
1396:}
1397:
1398:/**
1399: * The values a dropped set may leave at its path when nothing else edited it.
1400: * The first is the recorded value with every set written inside it later laid
1401: * on top. An older engine kept records in first-write order, so the recorded
1402: * value alone, and the recorded value with every dropped set inside it laid on,
1403: * are values the overlay may have left there too. A set the entry set still
1404: * names is masked out of the comparison, because it writes its value again
1405: * after the restore.
1406: * @param {object} record The dropped record.
1407: * @param {object[]} records Every record of the destination, in the order written.
1408: * @param {Array<Array<string|number>>} keptPaths Paths the entry set still sets.
1409: * @returns {{candidates: Array<*>, masks: Array<Array<string|number>>}} The
1410: *   values to compare with, and the locations to leave out of the comparison.
1411: */
1412:function valueLeftBySets(record, records, keptPaths) {
1413:  const droppedInside = records.filter((other) => isInsidePath(other.path, record.path) &&
1414:    !keptPaths.some((kept) => samePath(kept, other.path)));
1415:  const later = records.slice(records.indexOf(record) + 1);
1416:  const layOn = (inner) => inner.reduce((laid, other) => changedAt(laid, other.path.slice(record.path.length),
1417:    (container, step) => {
1418:      container[step] = other.value;
1419:    }), record.value);
1420:  const candidates = [layOn(droppedInside.filter((other) => later.includes(other))), record.value, layOn(droppedInside)];
1421:  const masks = keptPaths
1422:    .filter((kept) => isInsidePath(kept, record.path))
1423:    .map((kept) => kept.slice(record.path.length));
1424:  return { candidates, masks };
1425:}
1426:
1427:/**
1428: * Return every value a `set` wrote to what the pin holds once the entry set no
1429: * longer names its path, so a dropped `set` leaves the live file instead of
1430: * staying behind as a value nothing explains. A key the pin does not hold is
1431: * deleted while it still carries the recorded value. A value that changed after
1432: * it was applied is refused rather than overwritten: that edit belongs to a
1433: * person. Without a readable pinned copy there is no value to return to, so the
1434: * overlay is refused rather than given a guess. The same holds for a path
1435: * through an array whose items no longer line up with the pin's.
1436: * @param {string} text Live document text.
1437: * @param {object[]} operations Operations of one entry set, in order.
1438: * @param {object[]} recordedSets Sets this overlay applied to the destination.
1439: * @param {() => (string|null)} [readUpstreamText] Reads the destination as the
1440: *   pin holds it, or null when there is no upstream copy to read.
1441: * @param {object[]} [recordedItems] Items this overlay added to the destination.
1442: * @returns {string} The text with every dropped set taken back.
1443: */
1444:function restoreDroppedSets(text, operations, recordedSets, readUpstreamText = () => null, recordedItems = []) {
1445:  let output = text;
1446:  const records = lastRecordPerPath(recordedSets);
1447:  const keptPaths = operations.filter((operation) => operation.op === 'set').map((operation) => operation.path);
1448:  const dropped = records.filter((record) => !keptPaths.some((kept) => samePath(kept, record.path)));
1449:  // An outer value is taken back before a value written inside it. Once the
1450:  // outer value is the pin's, the inner one reads as the pin's too, so two
1451:  // nested sets dropped together both leave.
1452:  const outerFirst = [...dropped].sort((left, right) => left.path.length - right.path.length);
1453:  for (const record of outerFirst) {
1454:    const where = record.path.join('.');
1455:    const root = parseJsonSource(output);
1456:    // The key, or the object that held it, may be gone already: nothing is left
1457:    // for this record to take back. Through an array that reading waits for the
